<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_Saint_Pierre_et_Miquelon.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Map_Saint_Pierre_et_Miquelon.svg/220px-Map_Saint_Pierre_et_Miquelon.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="137" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Map_Saint_Pierre_et_Miquelon.svg/330px-Map_Saint_Pierre_et_Miquelon.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Map_Saint_Pierre_et_Miquelon.svg/440px-Map_Saint_Pierre_et_Miquelon.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="591" data-file-height="368" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_Saint_Pierre_et_Miquelon.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of Saint Pierre and Miquelon</div></div></div></div></div>
<p><b>Saint Pierre and Miquelon</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span><span title="/ɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;kit&#39;">ɪ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span></span>/</a></span></span>),<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> officially the <b>Territorial Collectivity of Saint-Pierre and Miquelon</b> (French: <i lang="fr">Collectivité territoriale de Saint-Pierre et Miquelon</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="fr-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/French" title="Help:IPA/French">[sɛ̃ pjɛʁ e miklɔ̃]</a></span>), is a self-governing territorial <a href="/wiki/Overseas_collectivity" title="Overseas collectivity">overseas collectivity</a> of <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> in the northwestern Atlantic Ocean near the <a href="/wiki/Canada" title="Canada">Canadian</a> province of <a href="/wiki/Newfoundland_and_Labrador" title="Newfoundland and Labrador">Newfoundland and Labrador</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> An <a href="/wiki/Archipelago" title="Archipelago">archipelago</a> of eight islands, Saint Pierre and Miquelon is a remaining vestige of the once-vast territory of <a href="/wiki/New_France" title="New France">New France</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Its residents are French citizens; the collectivity elects its own deputy to the <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_(France)" title="National Assembly (France)">National Assembly</a> and participates in senatorial and presidential elections. It covers 242&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (93&#160;sq&#160;mi) of land and had a population of 6,008 as of the March 2016 census<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-fn1_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fn1-1">&#91;note 1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-population_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-population-2">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The islands are in the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_St._Lawrence" title="Gulf of St. Lawrence">Gulf of St. Lawrence</a> near the entrance of <a href="/wiki/Fortune_Bay" title="Fortune Bay">Fortune Bay</a>, which extends into the southwestern coast of <a href="/wiki/Newfoundland_(island)" title="Newfoundland (island)">Newfoundland</a>, near the <a href="/wiki/Grand_Banks_of_Newfoundland" title="Grand Banks of Newfoundland">Grand Banks of Newfoundland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-iarc_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-iarc-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> St. Pierre is 19&#160;km (12&#160;mi) from <a href="/wiki/Point_May" title="Point May">Point May</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Burin_Peninsula" title="Burin Peninsula">Burin Peninsula</a> of Newfoundland and 3,819&#160;km (2,373&#160;mi) from <a href="/wiki/Brest,_France" title="Brest, France">Brest</a>, the nearest city in <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_France" title="Metropolitan France">Metropolitan France</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> The tiny Canadian <a href="/wiki/Green_Island_(Fortune),_Newfoundland_and_Labrador" title="Green Island (Fortune), Newfoundland and Labrador">Green Island</a> lies 10&#160;km (6&#160;mi) east of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Pierre_Island" title="Saint Pierre Island">St. Pierre</a>, roughly halfway to Point May.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Saint-Pierre</i></span> is French for <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Saint Peter</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of fishermen.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The present name of <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Miquelon</i></span> was first noted in the form of <i>Micquetô</i>, <i>Miqueton</i> or <i>Micquellon</i> in the French Basque sailor <a href="/wiki/Martin_de_Hoyar%C3%A7abal" title="Martin de Hoyarçabal">Martin de Hoyarçabal</a>'s 1579 navigational pilot for Newfoundland, <i><a href="/wiki/Les_voyages_aventureux_du_Capitaine_Martin_de_Hoyarsal,_habitant_du_%C3%A7ubiburu" class="mw-redirect" title="Les voyages aventureux du Capitaine Martin de Hoyarsal, habitant du çubiburu">Les voyages aventureux du Capitaine Martin de Hoyarsabal, habitant du çubiburu</a></i>: "Giſant le cap de Breton &amp; le pertuis de Miqueton est oest, y a 42 <a href="/wiki/League_(unit)" title="League (unit)">l</a>. [leagues]" ... "Gisant la Colombe de S. Pierre le pertuis de Micquellon nord noroest &amp; sud suest: y a 7 l.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> It has been claimed that the name <i>Miquelon</i> is a <a href="/wiki/Basque_language" title="Basque language">Basque</a> form of Michael; <i>Mikel</i> and <i>Mikels</i> are usually named <i>Mikelon</i> in the <a href="/wiki/Basque_Country_(autonomous_community)" title="Basque Country (autonomous community)">Basque Country</a>. Therefore, from <i>Mikelon</i> it may have been written in the French way with a <i>q</i> instead of a <i>k</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Cormier_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cormier-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-tourism_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tourism-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Basque Country is divided between Spain and France, and most Basques live south of <a href="/wiki/France%E2%80%93Spain_border" title="France–Spain border">the border</a>, so Miquelon may have been influenced by the Spanish name <span title="Spanish-language text"><i lang="es">Miguelón</i></span>, an augmentative form of Miguel meaning "big Michael". The adjoined island's name of "<a href="/wiki/Langlade_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Langlade Island">Langlade</a>" is said to be an adaptation of <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">l'île à l'Anglais</i></span> (Englishman's Island).<sup id="cite_ref-tourism_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tourism-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

class="thumbcaption">Saint Pierre in 1921</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Archaeological evidence indicates that indigenous peoples, such as the <a href="/wiki/Beothuk" title="Beothuk">Beothuk</a>, visited St Pierre and Miquelon, but it is not thought that they settled on the islands permanently.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> On 21 October 1520 the Portuguese explorer <a href="/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_%C3%81lvares_Fagundes" title="João Álvares Fagundes">João Álvares Fagundes</a> landed on the islands and named the St. Pierre island group the '<a href="/wiki/Eleven_Thousand_Virgins" class="mw-redirect" title="Eleven Thousand Virgins">Eleven Thousand Virgins</a>', as the day marked the feast day of <a href="/wiki/St._Ursula" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Ursula">St. Ursula</a> and her virgin companions.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> In 1536 <a href="/wiki/Jacques_Cartier" title="Jacques Cartier">Jacques Cartier</a> claimed the islands as a French possession on behalf of the King of France, <a href="/wiki/Francis_I_of_France" title="Francis I of France">Francis I</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Though already frequented by <a href="/wiki/Mi%27kmaq_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Mi&#39;kmaq people">Mi'kmaq people</a><sup id="cite_ref-gb-11_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-11-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> and by Basque and Breton fishermen,<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> the islands were not permanently settled until the end of the 17th century: four permanent inhabitants were counted in 1670, and 22 in 1691.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1670, during <a href="/wiki/Jean_Talon" title="Jean Talon">Jean Talon</a>'s second tenure as <a href="/wiki/Intendant_of_New_France" title="Intendant of New France">Intendant of New France</a>, a French officer annexed the islands after he discovered a dozen fishermen from France encamped there, naming them Saint Pierre and Miquelon. During <a href="/wiki/King_William%27s_War" title="King William&#39;s War">King William's War</a> and <a href="/wiki/Queen_Anne%27s_War" title="Queen Anne&#39;s War">Queen Anne's War</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_England" title="Kingdom of England">English</a> forces launched multiple attacks against French colonial settlements on the islands, and by the early 18th century the colonists had abandoned Saint Pierre and Miquelon altogether.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-11_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-11-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> In the 1713 <a href="/wiki/Peace_of_Utrecht" title="Peace of Utrecht">Treaty of Utrecht</a> which ended the <a href="/wiki/War_of_the_Spanish_Succession" title="War of the Spanish Succession">War of the Spanish Succession</a>, France ceded the islands to <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Great_Britain" title="Kingdom of Great Britain">Britain</a>. <sup id="cite_ref-gb-11_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-11-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The British renamed the island of Saint Pierre to Saint Peter, and small numbers of colonists from <a href="/wiki/Great_Britain" title="Great Britain">Great Britain</a> and <a href="/wiki/British_America" title="British America">Britain's American colonies</a> began to settle on the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Under the terms of the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_(1763)" title="Treaty of Paris (1763)">1763 Treaty of Paris</a>, which put an end to the <a href="/wiki/Seven_Years%27_War" title="Seven Years&#39; War">Seven Years' War</a>, France ceded all its <a href="/wiki/New_France" title="New France">North American possessions</a> to Britain, though the British granted <a href="/wiki/French_Shore" title="French Shore">fishing rights</a> to French fishermen along the Newfoundland coast, and as part of that arrangement returned Saint Pierre and Miquelon to France's control.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-12_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-12-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> After France <a href="/wiki/France_in_the_American_Revolutionary_War" title="France in the American Revolutionary War">entered</a> the <a href="/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War" title="American Revolutionary War">American Revolutionary War</a> on the side of the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a> and declared war on Britain, a British force invaded Saint Pierre and Miquelon and briefly occupied them, destroying all colonial settlements on the islands and deporting 2,000 colonists back to France.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-16_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-16-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> In 1793, during the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolutionary_Wars" title="French Revolutionary Wars">French Revolutionary Wars</a>, another British force landed in Saint Pierre and, in the following year, again deporting the French colonial population, and tried to establish a community of Anglophone settlers.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-11_16-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-11-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>The nascent British colony was in turn attacked by the <a href="/wiki/French_Navy" title="French Navy">French Navy</a> in 1796. The <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Amiens" title="Treaty of Amiens">Treaty of Amiens</a> of 1802 returned the islands to France, but Britain reoccupied them when hostilities recommenced the next year.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-11_16-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-11-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The 1814 <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_(1814)" title="Treaty of Paris (1814)">Treaty of Paris</a> gave the islands back to France, though the UK occupied them yet again during the <a href="/wiki/Hundred_Days" title="Hundred Days">Hundred Days</a> War in 1815. France then reclaimed the now uninhabited islands, in which all structures and buildings had been destroyed or fallen into disrepair.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-11_16-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-11-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The islands were resettled in 1816. The settlers, mostly Basques, Bretons and <a href="/wiki/Normans" title="Normans">Normans</a>, were joined by various other peoples, particularly from the nearby island of Newfoundland.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Only around the middle of the century did increased fishing bring a certain prosperity to the little colony.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-11_16-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-11-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="20th_century">20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: 20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1903 the colony toyed with the idea of joining the United States, but in the end nothing came of the idea.<sup id="cite_ref-willing_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-willing-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> During the early 1910s the colony suffered severely as a result of unprofitable fisheries, and large numbers of its people emigrated to <a href="/wiki/Nova_Scotia" title="Nova Scotia">Nova Scotia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Quebec" title="Quebec">Quebec</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-1911a_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1911a-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> The draft imposed on all male inhabitants of conscript age after the beginning of <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a> in 1914 crippled the fisheries, as their catch could not be processed by the older men or the women and children.<sup id="cite_ref-1911a_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1911a-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> About 400 men from the colony served in the <a href="/wiki/French_Armed_Forces" title="French Armed Forces">French military</a> during World War I (1914–1918), 25% of whom died.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-14_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-14-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The increase in the adoption of steam <a href="/wiki/Fishing_trawler" title="Fishing trawler"> trawlers</a> in the fisheries also contributed to the reduction in employment opportunities.<sup id="cite_ref-1911a_22-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1911a-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Smuggling had always been an important economic activity in the islands, but it became especially prominent in the 1920s with the institution of <a href="/wiki/Prohibition_in_the_United_States" title="Prohibition in the United States">Prohibition in the United States</a> from January 1920.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-14_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-14-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> In 1931 the archipelago was reported by the <i>New York Times</i> to have imported 1,815,271 U.S. gallons (1,511,529 <a href="/wiki/Imperial_gallon" class="mw-redirect" title="Imperial gallon">imperial gallons</a>; 6,871,550 <a href="/wiki/Litre" title="Litre">liters</a>) of whisky from Canada in 12 months, most of it to be smuggled into the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-pro-nyt_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pro-nyt-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The end of Prohibition in 1933 plunged the islands once more into economic depression.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc-sm_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-sm-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, despite opposition from Canada, Britain,<sup id="cite_ref-auto_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> and the United States, <a href="/wiki/Charles_de_Gaulle" title="Charles de Gaulle">Charles de Gaulle</a>'s forces <a href="/wiki/Capture_of_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Capture of Saint Pierre and Miquelon">seized the archipelago</a> from <a href="/wiki/Vichy_France" title="Vichy France">Vichy France</a>, to which the local administrator had pledged its allegiance, in December 1941. In referendums on both islands, the population endorsed the takeover by <a href="/wiki/Free_France" title="Free France">Free France</a> by over 98%.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-gb-15_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-15-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The colony became a <a href="/wiki/French_Overseas_Territory" class="mw-redirect" title="French Overseas Territory">French Overseas Territory</a> in 1946. After the <a href="/wiki/1958_French_constitutional_referendum" title="1958 French constitutional referendum">1958 French constitutional referendum</a>, the territory of Saint Pierre and Miquelon was asked to choose one of three options:  becoming fully integrated with France, becoming a self-governing state within the <a href="/wiki/French_Community" title="French Community">French Community</a>, or preserving the status of an overseas territory; it decided to remain a territory.<sup id="cite_ref-herald_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-herald-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Politics of Saint Pierre and Miquelon">Politics of Saint Pierre and Miquelon</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Flag_of_Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Flag_of_Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon.svg/220px-Flag_of_Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Flag_of_Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon.svg/330px-Flag_of_Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Flag_of_Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon.svg/440px-Flag_of_Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="450" data-file-height="300" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Flag_of_Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Locally used flag of Saint Pierre and Miquelon, modelled after the <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Coat of arms of Saint Pierre and Miquelon">coat of arms</a></div></div></div>
<p>Since March 2003, Saint Pierre and Miquelon has been an overseas collectivity with a special status.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The archipelago became an overseas territory in 1946, then an <a href="/wiki/Overseas_department" class="mw-redirect" title="Overseas department">overseas department</a> on 19 July 1976,<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> before acquiring the status of <a href="/wiki/Territorial_collectivity" title="Territorial collectivity">territorial collectivity</a> on 11 June 1985.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-rece-2006_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rece-2006-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> The archipelago has two <a href="/wiki/Communes_of_France" title="Communes of France">communes</a>: <a href="/wiki/Saint-Pierre,_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Saint-Pierre, Saint Pierre and Miquelon">Saint-Pierre</a> and <a href="/wiki/Miquelon-Langlade" title="Miquelon-Langlade">Miquelon-Langlade</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-sodepar-statut_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sodepar-statut-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> A third commune, <a href="/wiki/Isle-aux-Marins" class="mw-redirect" title="Isle-aux-Marins">Isle-aux-Marins</a>, existed until 1945, when it was absorbed by the municipality of Saint-Pierre.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The inhabitants possess <a href="/wiki/French_citizenship" class="mw-redirect" title="French citizenship">French citizenship</a> and suffrage.<sup id="cite_ref-brit-1_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-1-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Saint Pierre and Miquelon sends a senator and a <a href="/wiki/Saint-Pierre-et-Miquelon%27s_1st_constituency" title="Saint-Pierre-et-Miquelon&#39;s 1st constituency">deputy</a> to the <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="National Assembly of France">National Assembly of France</a> in Paris and enjoys a degree of autonomy concerning taxes, customs, and excise.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-16_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-16-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>France appoints the <a href="/wiki/Prefect_of_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Prefect of Saint Pierre and Miquelon">Prefect of Saint Pierre and Miquelon</a>, who represents the national government in the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc-sm_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-sm-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> The Prefect is in charge of national interests, law enforcement, public order, and, under the conditions set by the statute of 1985, administrative control.<sup id="cite_ref-pref_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pref-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Since January 2021, the current prefect is Christian Pouget.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The local legislative body, the <a href="/wiki/Territorial_Council_of_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Territorial Council of Saint Pierre and Miquelon">Territorial Council</a> (French: <i lang="fr">Conseil territorial</i>), has 19 members: four councillors from Miquelon-Langlade and 15 from Saint-Pierre.<sup id="cite_ref-sodepar-statut_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sodepar-statut-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The President of the Territorial Council is the head of a delegation of "France in the name of Saint Pierre and Miquelon" for international events such as the annual meetings of <a href="/wiki/Northwest_Atlantic_Fisheries_Organization" title="Northwest Atlantic Fisheries Organization">NAFO</a> and <a href="/wiki/ICCAT" class="mw-redirect" title="ICCAT">ICCAT</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-sodepar-statut_33-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sodepar-statut-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>France is responsible for the defence of the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/List_of_active_French_Navy_ships" title="List of active French Navy ships">French Navy</a> has maintained a patrol boat, the ex-trawler <a href="/wiki/List_of_active_French_Navy_ships#Coastguard" title="List of active French Navy ships">Fulmar</a>, in the region since 1997.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Law enforcement in Saint Pierre and Miquelon">Law enforcement in Saint Pierre and Miquelon</a> is the responsibility of a branch of the French <a href="/wiki/National_Gendarmerie" title="National Gendarmerie">Gendarmerie Nationale</a>; there are two police stations in the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-Prefecture_site_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Prefecture_site-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 10 January 2022 Saint Pierre and Miquelon made international news when <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament_(France)" class="mw-redirect" title="Member of Parliament (France)">MP</a> <a href="/wiki/St%C3%A9phane_Claireaux" title="Stéphane Claireaux">Stéphane Claireaux</a>, a member of the governing La République en Marche (LREM) was pelted with seaweed and stones in response to the government's new COVID-19 rules. The rule was announced by the state representative, the prefect, on 2 January for the island and angered residents.<sup id="cite_ref-Guardian_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guardian-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Maritime_boundary_case">Maritime boundary case</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Maritime boundary case">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Canada%E2%80%93France_Maritime_Boundary_Case" title="Canada–France Maritime Boundary Case">Canada–France Maritime Boundary Case</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon_EEZ_map-en.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon_EEZ_map-en.svg/220px-Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon_EEZ_map-en.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="235" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon_EEZ_map-en.svg/330px-Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon_EEZ_map-en.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon_EEZ_map-en.svg/440px-Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon_EEZ_map-en.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="750" data-file-height="800" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon_EEZ_map-en.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of the <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> of Saint Pierre and Miquelon</div></div></div>
<p>France claimed a 200-mile (320&#160;km) <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> for Saint-Pierre and Miquelon, and in August 1983 the naval ship <i><a href="/wiki/D%27Estienne_d%27Orves-class_aviso" title="D&#39;Estienne d&#39;Orves-class aviso">Lieutenant de vaisseau Le Hénaff</a></i> and the seismic ship <i>Lucien Beaufort</i> were sent to explore for oil in the disputed zone.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-13_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-13-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> In addition to the potential oil reserves, <a href="/wiki/Cod_fishing_in_Newfoundland" title="Cod fishing in Newfoundland">cod fishing</a> rights on the <a href="/wiki/Grand_Banks_of_Newfoundland" title="Grand Banks of Newfoundland">Grand Banks of Newfoundland</a> were at stake in the dispute. In the late 1980s, indications of declining fish stocks began to raise serious concern over the depletion of the fishery.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-13_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-13-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> In 1992, an arbitration panel awarded the islands an exclusive economic zone of 12,348 square kilometres (4,768&#160;sq&#160;mi) to settle a longstanding territorial dispute with Canada, although it represents only 25% of what France had sought.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 1992 decision fixed the maritime boundaries between Canada and the islands, but did not demarcate the <a href="/wiki/Continental_shelf" title="Continental shelf">continental shelf</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-economist_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-economist-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Geography of Saint Pierre and Miquelon">Geography of Saint Pierre and Miquelon</a></div>
<p>Located off the western end of the Newfoundland's <a href="/wiki/Burin_Peninsula" title="Burin Peninsula">Burin Peninsula</a>, the archipelago of Saint Pierre and Miquelon comprises eight islands, totalling 242 square kilometres (93&#160;sq&#160;mi), of which only two are inhabited.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ann-2010-1_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ann-2010-1-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> The islands are bare and rocky, with steep coasts, and only a thin layer of <a href="/wiki/Peat" title="Peat">peat</a> to soften the hard landscape.<sup id="cite_ref-htg_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-htg-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> The islands, like Newfoundland, are geologically part of the northeastern end of the <a href="/wiki/Appalachian_Mountains" title="Appalachian Mountains">Appalachian Mountains</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Miquelon-Langlade</i></span>, the largest island, is in fact composed of two islands; <a href="/wiki/Miquelon_Island" title="Miquelon Island">Miquelon Island</a> (also called <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><b>Grande Miquelon</b></i></span>, 110&#160;km<sup>2</sup> or 42&#160;sq&#160;mi) is connected to Langlade Island (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><b>Petite Miquelon</b></i></span>, 91&#160;km<sup>2</sup> or 35&#160;sq&#160;mi) by the <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/La_Dune" class="mw-redirect" title="La Dune">Dune de Langlade</a></i></span></i> (also known as the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><b>Isthme de Langlade</b></i></span>), a 10-kilometre (6.2&#160;mi) long sandy <a href="/wiki/Tombolo" title="Tombolo">tombolo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-rece-2006_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rece-2006-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> A storm severed them in the 18th century, separating the two islands for several decades, before currents reconstructed the isthmus.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Morne_de_la_Grande_Montagne" title="Morne de la Grande Montagne">Morne de la Grande Montagne</a></i></span>, the tallest point in the territory at 240 meters high, is located on Grande Miquelon.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The waters between Langlade and Saint-Pierre were called "the Mouth of Hell" (French: <i lang="fr">Gueule d'Enfer</i>) until about 1900, as more than 600 shipwrecks have been recorded in that point since 1800.<sup id="cite_ref-maplepedia_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maplepedia-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> In the north of Miquelon Island is the village of <a href="/wiki/Miquelon-Langlade" title="Miquelon-Langlade">Miquelon-Langlade</a> (710 inhabitants), while Langlade Island is almost deserted (only one inhabitant in the 1999 census).<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Saint_Pierre_Island" title="Saint Pierre Island">Saint Pierre Island</a></i></span>, whose area is smaller, 26 square kilometres (10&#160;sq&#160;mi), is the most populous and the commercial and administrative center of the archipelago. <a href="/wiki/Saint-Pierre_Airport" title="Saint-Pierre Airport">Saint-Pierre Airport</a> has been in operation since 1999 and is capable of accommodating long-haul flights from France.<sup id="cite_ref-rece-2006_32-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rece-2006-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A third, formerly inhabited island, <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/L%27%C3%8Ele-aux-Marins" title="L&#39;Île-aux-Marins">Isle-aux-Marins</a></i></span>, known as <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Île-aux-Chiens</i></span> until 1931 and located a short distance from the port of Saint-Pierre, has been uninhabited since 1963.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The other main islands are <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Grand_Colombier" title="Grand Colombier">Grand Colombier</a></i></span>, <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/%C3%8Ele_aux_Vainqueurs" title="Île aux Vainqueurs">Île aux Vainqueurs</a></i></span>, and <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/%C3%8Ele_aux_Pigeons" title="Île aux Pigeons">Île aux Pigeons</a></i></span>.
</p>
<ul class="gallery mw-gallery-traditional">

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment">Environment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Environment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/South_Avalon%E2%80%93Burin_oceanic_barrens" title="South Avalon–Burin oceanic barrens">South Avalon–Burin oceanic barrens</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Seabirds" class="mw-redirect" title="Seabirds">Seabirds</a> are the most common fauna.<sup id="cite_ref-brit-1_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-1-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pinniped" title="Pinniped">Seals</a> and other wildlife can be found in the Grand Barachois Lagoon of Miquelon. Every spring, whales migrating to <a href="/wiki/Greenland" title="Greenland">Greenland</a> are visible off the coasts of Saint-Pierre and Miquelon. <a href="/wiki/Trilobite" title="Trilobite">Trilobite</a> fossils have been found on Langlade. The stone pillars off the island coasts called "L'anse aux Soldats" eroded away and disappeared in the 1970s.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The rocky islands are barren, except for scrubby <a href="/wiki/Taxus_canadensis" title="Taxus canadensis">yews</a> and <a href="/wiki/Juniper" title="Juniper">junipers</a> and thin volcanic soil.<sup id="cite_ref-maplepedia_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maplepedia-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> The forest cover of the hills, except in parts of Langlade, had been removed for fuel long ago.<sup id="cite_ref-brit-1_34-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-1-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Port_of_saint-pierre,_SPM.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Port_of_saint-pierre%2C_SPM.jpg/220px-Port_of_saint-pierre%2C_SPM.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Port_of_saint-pierre%2C_SPM.jpg/330px-Port_of_saint-pierre%2C_SPM.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Port_of_saint-pierre%2C_SPM.jpg/440px-Port_of_saint-pierre%2C_SPM.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Port_of_saint-pierre,_SPM.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Port of Miquelon during the winter</div></div></div>
<p>In spite of being located at a similar latitude to the <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Biscay" title="Bay of Biscay">Bay of Biscay</a>, the archipelago is characterized by a cold borderline <a href="/wiki/Humid_continental_climate" title="Humid continental climate">humid continental</a>/<a href="/wiki/Subarctic_climate" title="Subarctic climate">subarctic climate</a>, under the influence of polar air masses and the cold <a href="/wiki/Labrador_Current" title="Labrador Current">Labrador Current</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ann-2010-1_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ann-2010-1-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> The mild winters for being a subarctic climate also means it has influences of <a href="/wiki/Subpolar_oceanic_climate" class="mw-redirect" title="Subpolar oceanic climate">subpolar oceanic climate</a>, thus being at the confluence of three climatic types. The February mean is just below the −3&#160;°C (27&#160;°F) isotherm for that classification.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Due to just three months being above 10&#160;°C (50&#160;°F) in mean temperatures and winter lows being so mild, Saint Pierre and Miquelon has a <a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_Climate_Classification" class="mw-redirect" title="Köppen Climate Classification">Köppen Climate Classification</a> of <i>Dfc</i>, if bordering on <i>Cfc</i> due to the mildness of the winter and either <i>Dfb</i> or <i>Cfb</i> due to the closeness of the fourth-and fifth-warmest months to having mean temperatures at or above 10&#160;°C (50&#160;°F).
</p><p>Typical maritime <a href="/wiki/Seasonal_lag" title="Seasonal lag">seasonal lag</a> is also strong with September being warmer than June and March being colder than December. The average temperature is 5.3&#160;°C (41.5&#160;°F), with a temperature range of 19&#160;°C (34&#160;°F) between the warmest (15.7&#160;°C (60.3&#160;°F) in August) and coldest months (−3.6&#160;°C (25.5&#160;°F) in February).<sup id="cite_ref-ann-2010-1_42-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ann-2010-1-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Precipitation_(meteorology)" class="mw-redirect" title="Precipitation (meteorology)">Precipitation</a> is abundant (1,312&#160;mm or 51.7&#160;in per year) and regular (146 days per year), falling as snow and rain.<sup id="cite_ref-ann-2010-1_42-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ann-2010-1-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Because of its location at the confluence of the cold waters of the Labrador Current and the warm waters of the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_Stream" title="Gulf Stream">Gulf Stream</a>, the archipelago is also crossed a hundred days a year by fog banks, mainly in June and July.<sup id="cite_ref-ann-2010-1_42-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ann-2010-1-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Two other climatic elements are remarkable: the extremely variable winds and haze during the spring to early summer.<sup id="cite_ref-om-pres_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-om-pres-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Economy of Saint Pierre and Miquelon">Economy of Saint Pierre and Miquelon</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:432px;max-width:432px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:199px;max-width:199px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:147px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint-Pierre_harbor.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Saint-Pierre_harbor.jpg/197px-Saint-Pierre_harbor.jpg" decoding="async" width="197" height="148" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Saint-Pierre_harbor.jpg/296px-Saint-Pierre_harbor.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Saint-Pierre_harbor.jpg/394px-Saint-Pierre_harbor.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Fishing boats in Saint-Pierre harbour</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:229px;max-width:229px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:147px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/227px-Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="227" height="148" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/341px-Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/454px-Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">A proportional representation of Saint Pierre and Miquelon exports, 2019</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>The inhabitants have traditionally earned their livelihood by fishing and by servicing the fishing fleets operating off the coast of Newfoundland.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The climate and the small amount of available land militate against activities such as farming and livestock raising (weather conditions are severe, confining the <a href="/wiki/Growing_season" title="Growing season">growing season</a> to a few weeks, and the soil contains significant <a href="/wiki/Peat" title="Peat">peat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Clay" title="Clay">clay</a> and is largely infertile).<sup id="cite_ref-om-eco_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-om-eco-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Since 1992 the economy has been in steep decline, following the depletion of fish stocks due to <a href="/wiki/Overfishing" title="Overfishing">overfishing</a>, the limitation of fishing areas and the ban imposed on all cod fishing by the Canadian Government.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc-cod_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-cod-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The rise in unemployment has been countered by state financial aid for the retraining of businesses and individuals. The construction of the airport in 1999 helped sustain activity in the construction industry and public works.<sup id="cite_ref-rece-2006_32-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rece-2006-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Fish farming, crab fishing and agriculture are being developed to diversify the local economy.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_5-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The future of Saint Pierre and Miquelon rests on tourism, fisheries and <a href="/wiki/Aquaculture" title="Aquaculture">aquaculture</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Explorations are under way to exploit deposits of oil and gas.<sup id="cite_ref-rece-2006_32-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rece-2006-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Tourism benefits from the proximity to similar tourist areas of Canada.  Distribution, public service, care, minor wholesale, retail and crafts are notable in the <a href="/wiki/Business_sector" title="Business sector">business sector</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-om-eco_53-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-om-eco-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The labour market is characterized by high <a href="/wiki/Seasonality" title="Seasonality">seasonality</a>, due to climatic hazards. Traditionally, the inhabitants suspended all outdoor activities (construction, agriculture, etc.) 
between December and April.<sup id="cite_ref-ann-2010-3_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ann-2010-3-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> In 1999 the unemployment rate was 12.8%, and a third of the employed worked in the public sector. The employment situation was worsened by the complete cessation of deep-sea fishing, the traditional occupation of the islanders, as the unemployment rate in 1990 was lower at 9.5%.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The unemployment for 2010 shows a decrease from 2009, from 7.7% to 7.1%.<sup id="cite_ref-ann-2010-3_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ann-2010-3-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Exports are very low (5.1% of GDP) while imports are significant (49.1% of GDP).<sup id="cite_ref-pib-v-32_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pib-v-32-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> About 70% of the islands' supplies are imported from Canada or from other parts of France via Nova Scotia.<sup id="cite_ref-brit-1_34-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-1-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Stamp-St_Pierre_1891_25Fr_overprint.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Stamp-St_Pierre_1891_25Fr_overprint.jpg/170px-Stamp-St_Pierre_1891_25Fr_overprint.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="201" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Stamp-St_Pierre_1891_25Fr_overprint.jpg/255px-Stamp-St_Pierre_1891_25Fr_overprint.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Stamp-St_Pierre_1891_25Fr_overprint.jpg/340px-Stamp-St_Pierre_1891_25Fr_overprint.jpg 2x" data-file-width="367" data-file-height="434" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Stamp-St_Pierre_1891_25Fr_overprint.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>An 1891 <a href="/wiki/Postage_stamps_and_postal_history_of_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Postage stamps and postal history of Saint Pierre and Miquelon">postage stamp</a> from Saint Pierre and Miquelon</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Euro" title="Euro">euro</a> is the currency in Saint Pierre and Miquelon.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Canadian_dollar" title="Canadian dollar">Canadian dollar</a> is also widely accepted and used.<sup id="cite_ref-wkcg_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wkcg-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> The "Institut d'émission des départements d'outre-mer" (IEDOM), the French public institution responsible for issuing currency in the overseas territories that use the euro on behalf of the <a href="/wiki/Bank_of_France" title="Bank of France">Bank of France</a>, has had an agency in Saint Pierre since 1978.<sup id="cite_ref-iedom-sp_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-iedom-sp-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> The islands have issued <a href="/wiki/Postage_stamps_and_postal_history_of_St._Pierre_and_Miquelon" class="mw-redirect" title="Postage stamps and postal history of St. Pierre and Miquelon">their own stamps</a> from 1885 to the present, except for a period between 1 April 1978 and 3 February 1986 when French stamps not specific to Saint Pierre and Miquelon were used.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

(1847–1962;<sup id="cite_ref-hist-1847-1962_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hist-1847-1962-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> 1967–2011;<sup id="cite_ref-hist-1967-2011_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hist-1967-2011-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> 2016<sup id="cite_ref-population_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-population-2">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>)</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The total population of the islands at the March 2016 census was 6,008,<sup id="cite_ref-fn1_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fn1-1">&#91;note 1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-population_2-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-population-2">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> of which 5,412 lived in <a href="/wiki/Saint-Pierre,_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Saint-Pierre, Saint Pierre and Miquelon">Saint-Pierre</a> and 596 in <a href="/wiki/Miquelon-Langlade" title="Miquelon-Langlade">Miquelon-Langlade</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> At the time of the 1999 census, 76% of the population was born on the archipelago, while 16.1% were born in metropolitan France, a sharp increase from the 10.2% in 1990. In the same census, less than 1% of the population reported being a foreign national.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The archipelago has a high emigration rate, especially among young adults, who often leave for their studies without returning afterwards.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Even at the time of the great prosperity of the cod fishery, the population growth had always been constrained by the geographic remoteness, harsh climate and infertile soils.<sup id="cite_ref-1999-insee-731_15-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1999-insee-731-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnography">Ethnography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Ethnography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Ruins show that <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas" title="Indigenous peoples of the Americas">Indigenous American</a> people visited the archipelago on fishing and hunting expeditions before it was colonized by Europeans.<sup id="cite_ref-om-pres_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-om-pres-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The current population is the result of inflows of settlers from the French ports, mostly <a href="/wiki/Normans" title="Normans">Normans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Basque_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Basque people">Basques</a>, <a href="/wiki/Breton_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Breton people">Bretons</a> and <a href="/wiki/Saintonge_(region)" title="Saintonge (region)">Saintongeais</a>, and also from the historic area of <a href="/wiki/Acadia" title="Acadia">Acadia</a> in Canada (<a href="/wiki/Gasp%C3%A9_Peninsula" title="Gaspé Peninsula">Gaspé Peninsula</a>, parts of <a href="/wiki/New_Brunswick" title="New Brunswick">New Brunswick</a>, Prince Edward Island and <a href="/wiki/Cape_Breton" class="mw-redirect" title="Cape Breton">Cape Breton</a>) as well as <a href="/wiki/Newfoundland_French" title="Newfoundland French">Francophones</a> who settled on the <a href="/wiki/Port_au_Port_Peninsula" title="Port au Port Peninsula">Port au Port Peninsula</a> on Newfoundland.<sup id="cite_ref-om-pres_47-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-om-pres-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The inhabitants speak French; their customs and traditions are similar to the ones found in metropolitan France.<sup id="cite_ref-brit-1_34-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-1-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The French spoken on the archipelago is closer to <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_French" class="mw-redirect" title="Metropolitan French">Metropolitan French</a> than to <a href="/wiki/Canadian_French" title="Canadian French">Canadian French</a> and maintains a number of unique features.<sup id="cite_ref-frpm_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-frpm-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Basque, formerly spoken in private settings by people of Basque ancestry, had disappeared from the islands by the late 1950s.<sup id="cite_ref-bq-l_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bq-l-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The population is overwhelmingly Christian,<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> with the majority being Roman Catholic.<sup id="cite_ref-brit-1_34-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit-1-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Vicariate_Apostolic_of_Iles_Saint-Pierre_and_Miquelon" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Vicariate Apostolic of Iles Saint-Pierre and Miquelon">Roman Catholic Vicariate Apostolic of Iles Saint-Pierre and Miquelon</a> used to manage the local church until it was merged into the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_La_Rochelle_and_Saintes" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of La Rochelle and Saintes">French diocese of La Rochelle and Saintes</a> in 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Every summer there is a Basque Festival, which has demonstrations of  <i>harrijasotzaile</i> (stone heaving), <i>aizkolari</i> (lumberjack skills), and <a href="/wiki/Basque_pelota" title="Basque pelota">Basque pelota</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-zazpiak_bat_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-zazpiak_bat-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The local cuisine is mostly based on seafood such as lobster, snow crab, mussels, and especially cod.<sup id="cite_ref-adom_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-adom-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Ice_hockey" title="Ice hockey">Ice hockey</a> is very popular in Saint-Pierre and Miquelon, with local teams often competing in Newfoundland-based leagues. The crew of the Fulmar, a patrol ship stationed in the islands, formed an ice hockey team, "O.K Fulmar".<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Several players from the islands have played on French and Canadian teams and even participated on <a href="/wiki/France_men%27s_national_ice_hockey_team" title="France men&#39;s national ice hockey team">France men's national ice hockey team</a> in the Olympics.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Street names are not commonly used on the islands. Directions and locations are commonly given using nicknames and the names of nearby residents.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The only time the <a href="/wiki/Guillotine" title="Guillotine">guillotine</a> was used in North America was on Saint-Pierre in the late 19th century.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific page or range of pages in which the material appears. (October 2020)">page&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Joseph Néel was convicted of killing Mr Coupard on Île aux Chiens on 30 December 1888, and subsequently executed by <a href="/wiki/Guillotine" title="Guillotine">guillotine</a> on 24 August 1889. The device had to be shipped from the French territory of <a href="/wiki/Martinique" title="Martinique">Martinique</a> and it did not arrive in working order. It was very difficult to get anyone to perform the execution; finally a recent immigrant was coaxed into doing the job. This event was the inspiration for the 2000 film <i><a href="/wiki/The_Widow_of_Saint-Pierre" title="The Widow of Saint-Pierre">The Widow of Saint-Pierre</a></i>. The guillotine is now in a museum in Saint-Pierre.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_72-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific page or range of pages in which the material appears. (October 2020)">page&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transportation">Transportation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Transportation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon" title="Transport in Saint Pierre and Miquelon">Transport in Saint Pierre and Miquelon</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Miquelon_Airport.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/49/Miquelon_Airport.JPG/220px-Miquelon_Airport.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/49/Miquelon_Airport.JPG/330px-Miquelon_Airport.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/49/Miquelon_Airport.JPG/440px-Miquelon_Airport.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Miquelon_Airport.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Miquelon_Airport" title="Miquelon Airport">Miquelon Airport</a></div></div></div>
<p>For many years there was no direct air link between Saint Pierre and mainland France. Although the 1999 opening of the <a href="/wiki/Saint-Pierre_Airport" title="Saint-Pierre Airport">Saint-Pierre Airport</a> was intended to overcome this problem, a direct air link was not established until <a href="/wiki/Air_Saint-Pierre" title="Air Saint-Pierre">Air Saint-Pierre</a> announced it would conduct direct seasonal flights from Paris in the summer of 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Until then, all flights from and to Saint-Pierre passed through Canada. Air Saint-Pierre's <a href="/wiki/ATR_42" title="ATR 42">ATR 42</a> aircraft flies seasonally from the Canadian airports of <a href="/wiki/Sydney/J.A._Douglas_McCurdy_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Sydney/J.A. Douglas McCurdy Airport">Sydney</a> and <a href="/wiki/Stephenville_International_Airport" title="Stephenville International Airport">Stephenville</a>, and year-round from <a href="/wiki/Halifax_Stanfield_International_Airport" title="Halifax Stanfield International Airport">Halifax</a>, <a href="/wiki/Montr%C3%A9al-Pierre_Elliott_Trudeau_International_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Montréal-Pierre Elliott Trudeau International Airport">Montreal</a>, and <a href="/wiki/St._John%27s_International_Airport" title="St. John&#39;s International Airport">St John's</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-tourisme_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tourisme-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> A smaller <a href="/wiki/Miquelon_Airport" title="Miquelon Airport">airport on Miquelon</a> provides inter-island flights.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Ferry services operated by <a href="/w/index.php?title=SPM_Ferries&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="SPM Ferries (page does not exist)">SPM Ferries</a><sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> connect Saint Pierre with Miquelon and the Newfoundland town of <a href="/wiki/Fortune,_Newfoundland_and_Labrador" title="Fortune, Newfoundland and Labrador">Fortune</a>. In the summer, additional services operate between St Pierre and Langlade and between Miquelon and Fortune.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> Due to <a href="/wiki/Covid-19" class="mw-redirect" title="Covid-19">COVID-19</a> restrictions, services to Fortune were suspended between March 2020<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> and August 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> The ferries are capable of carrying up to 188 passengers and 18 vehicles.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Communications">Communications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Communications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Saint-Pierre and Miquelon have four radio stations; all stations operate on the <a href="/wiki/FM_radio" class="mw-redirect" title="FM radio">FM band</a>, with the last stations converted from the <a href="/wiki/AM_radio" class="mw-redirect" title="AM radio">AM band</a> in 2004. Three of the stations are on Saint-Pierre, two of which are owned by <a href="/wiki/Outre-Mer_1%C3%A8re" class="mw-redirect" title="Outre-Mer 1ère">Outre-Mer 1ère</a>, along with one 1ère station on Miquelon. At night, these stations broadcast <a href="/wiki/France-Inter" class="mw-redirect" title="France-Inter">France-Inter</a>. The other station (Radio Atlantique) is an affiliate of <a href="/wiki/Radio_France_Internationale" title="Radio France Internationale">Radio France Internationale</a>. The nation is linked to North America and Europe by <a href="/wiki/Satellite" title="Satellite">satellite</a> communications for telephone and television service.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The department of Saint-Pierre and Miquelon is served by three television stations: Saint-Pierre-et-Miquelon 1ère (<a href="/wiki/Call_letter" class="mw-redirect" title="Call letter">call letters</a> FQN) on Channel 8, with a repeater on Channel 31, and <a href="/wiki/France_%C3%94" title="France Ô">France Ô</a> on Channel 6. While Saint-Pierre and Miquelon use the French <a href="/wiki/SECAM" title="SECAM">SECAM</a>-K1 <a href="/wiki/Broadcast_television_systems" title="Broadcast television systems">standard for television broadcasts</a>, the local telecommunications provider (SPM Telecom) carries many North American television stations and cable channels, converted from North America's <a href="/wiki/NTSC" title="NTSC">NTSC</a> standard. In addition, Saint-Pierre-et-Miquelon 1ère is carried on <a href="/wiki/Shaw_Direct" title="Shaw Direct">Shaw Direct</a> satellite and most digital cable services in Canada, converted to NTSC.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>SPM Telecom is also the department's main <a href="/wiki/Internet_service_provider" title="Internet service provider">internet service provider</a>, with its internet service being named Cheznoo (a play on Chez-Nous, French for "Our Place"). SPM Telecom also offers cellular phone and mobile phone service (for phones that adhere to the <a href="/wiki/GSM" title="GSM">GSM</a> standard). SPM Telecom uses the GSM 900&#160;MHz band,<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> which is different from the GSM 850&#160;MHz and 1900&#160;MHz bands used in the rest of North America.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The islands are a well-known separate country-level entity among many radio amateurs, identifiable with ITU prefix "FP". Those visiting, mainly from the US, activate Saint-Pierre and Miquelon every year on amateur frequencies. Amateurs collect (records of) contacts with these stations for <a href="/wiki/Islands_on_the_Air" class="mw-redirect" title="Islands on the Air">Islands on the Air</a> and <a href="/wiki/DX_Century_Club" class="mw-redirect" title="DX Century Club">DX Century Club</a> awards; the Atlantic coast gives great takeoff for shortwaves. A few miles away is <a href="/wiki/Signal_Hill,_St._John%27s" title="Signal Hill, St. John&#39;s">Signal Hill, St. John's</a> which first communicated across the Atlantic, namely with <a href="/wiki/Poldhu#Marconi&#39;s_Poldhu_Wireless_Station" title="Poldhu">Marconi's Poldhu Wireless Station</a> in England.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="News">News</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: News">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>SPM Telecom publishes local news online at the Cheznoo web portal.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Other publications include the magazine "Mathurin".<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The archipelago has four primary schools (Sainte Odile, Henriette Bonin, Feu Rouge, les Quatre-Temps), one middle school (Collège de Miquelon/Collège Saint-Christophe) with an annex in Miquelon, one state (government) high school (<a href="/wiki/Lyc%C3%A9e-coll%C3%A8ge_d%E2%80%99Etat_Emile_Letournel" class="mw-redirect" title="Lycée-collège d’Etat Emile Letournel">Lycée-collège d’Etat Emile Letournel</a>) and one vocational high school.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The students who wish to further their studies after high school are granted access to scholarships to study overseas. Most students go to metropolitan France, although some go to Canada, mainly <a href="/wiki/New_Brunswick" title="New Brunswick">New Brunswick</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-profil-pm_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-profil-pm-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Saint Pierre's institute for higher learning is the Institut Frecker, which is associated with <a href="/wiki/Memorial_University_of_Newfoundland" title="Memorial University of Newfoundland">Memorial University of Newfoundland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Since 2000 Frecker had been operated by the Government of Saint Pierre and Miquelon, with support of the federal government of Canada and the provincial government of Newfoundland and Labrador.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Saint-Pierre and Miquelon's health care system is entirely public and free.<sup id="cite_ref-profil-pm_86-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-profil-pm-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> In 1994 France and Canada signed an agreement allowing the residents of the archipelago to be treated in <a href="/wiki/St._John%27s,_Newfoundland_and_Labrador" title="St. John&#39;s, Newfoundland and Labrador">St. John's</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-profil-pm_86-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-profil-pm-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> In 2015 St-Pierre and Miquelon indicated that they would start looking for a new healthcare provider as recent rate increases by <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Health" title="Eastern Health">Eastern Health</a> in Newfoundland were too expensive (increasing to $3.3&#160;million in 2014 from $2.5&#160;million in 2010). <a href="/wiki/Halifax,_Nova_Scotia" title="Halifax, Nova Scotia">Halifax, Nova Scotia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Moncton" title="Moncton">Moncton</a>, New Brunswick were mooted as possible locations.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Since 1985 Hôpital François Dunan provides basic care and emergency care for residents of both islands.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> The island's first hospital was military in 1904 and became a civilian facility in 1905. L'Hôpital-Hospice-Orphelinat opened in 1937.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Fire_services">Fire services</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Fire services">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Fire stations:
</p>
<ul><li>Both airports, St Pierre and Miquelon, separately</li>
<li>Service incendie Ville de St Pierre – Caserne Renaissance has five apparatuses: 2 <a href="/wiki/Fire_engine" title="Fire engine">pumpers</a>, aerial ladders and a <a href="/wiki/Hazardous_materials_apparatus" title="Hazardous materials apparatus">hazmat</a>. This replaced Caserne Daguerre.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Service incendie Miquelon has four apparatuses:<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> aerial, hazmat, two pumpers</li></ul>
<p>Most are second-hand units from North America but<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> St Pierre acquired an aerial ladder from France in 2016.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Pierre_and_Miquelon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
